**Verified current path:** giftMediaClient performs metadata intention POST, presigned raw browser PUT, then empty-JSON finalize. giftMediaStorage sends raw bytes into stagingBucket; finalizeGiftMediaUpload claims a row/intent, HEAD/GETs those raw bytes, uses decodeGiftImage, marks durable UNKNOWN before publishing WebP to publicBucket, then rechecks membership/READY/site/lease and associates by CAS. Equal bucket names or a staging prefix would remove the original validation boundary. A public R2 domain exposes bucket contents; a prefix is not an access-control boundary. The proposed process writes only validated derivatives, and existing-bucket contents still need a separate authorized inventory before any public-access change. [Cloudflare public buckets](https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/buckets/public-buckets/).

The real BFF is apps/admin/src/server/apiProxy.ts: every non-GET/HEAD currently calls request.clone().arrayBuffer(), without a byte bound or forwarded signal. It preserves fixed-target path/origin/cookie rules and native-auth secret sanitization, but is not a bounded upload transport. The API JSON reader is16KiB and the current router checks native session/Origin, then the service supplies manageCatalog/READY/current-site checks. index.ts drops already-parsed config.giftMedia; A owns that separate composition correction. Sharp0.35.4 and S3 SDK3.1149.0 are installed; reuse them without manifest changes. The decoder already rejects unsupported signatures/formats, animation/truncation and input dimension/pixel excess, reencodes WebP inside2048 with no enlargement and strips metadata. Neither keys nor media composition can fabricate READY.

**Candidate wire, keeping the existing two route identities; not accepted C1:** metadata POST /v1/sites/:siteId/gift-media/uploads returns strict {upload:{id,expiresAt}}. Add required contentSha256 (64 lowercase hex) to the existing MIME/byte metadata; this is an untrusted content expectation, not decoder/provider proof. The client computes it from its bounded selected File before requesting the lease; the server compares the actual complete binary SHA256 before any decode/publication. The existing POST /v1/sites/:siteId/gift-media/:assetId/finalize handles three exact content-type/body cases: JPEG/PNG/WebP binary performs the bounded original upload/retry and returns unchanged strict {media}; JSON{} is an explicit read-only consultation; JSON{action:"RECOVER"} explicitly reconciles only the original possibly published derivative, without raw bytes, decode or PUT. A owns the strict status/recovery C1 alongside its already proposed lease change. No additional app route, credentials/capability in URL, base64, API URL disguised as putUrl, presigner fallback or browser R2 PUT remains.

Proposed private status envelope: {status:{siteId,assetId,state,expiresAt,canRetrySameBytes,media}}, strictly correlated; state is PENDING/PROCESSING/FINALIZED/FAILED/EXPIRED/UNKNOWN and media is null except FINALIZED. UNKNOWN is a projection over retained publication evidence, not a new database enum. No key, namespace, hash, processing ID, credentials or signed URL is exposed. JSON consultation performs no decode/storage write/association/claim; current native access and configured site/readiness rules still gate it. RECOVER obtains a fenced recovery lease and may perform bounded derivative HEAD/GET and final association only after exact stored content/scope checks; a missing/unavailable/mismatched object remains UNKNOWN. After upload expiry no raw decode/new PUT is authorized; recovery may associate an already existing exact original object, otherwise uncertainty/history remain retained. Existing admin origins are parent-provided values, not verified env; same-origin BFF carries the cookie and fixed Origin to the API. R2 browser PUT CORS is obsolete; any GET/HEAD CORS is only for actual cross-origin browser reads, not an upload authorization rule. [Cloudflare CORS](https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/buckets/cors/).

**Bounded transport and resources:** replace arrayBuffer only for this exact binary finalize path with a pull-driven bounded stream and linked request/upstream abort. Reject malformed/over-limit Content-Length if present, count actual chunks before forwarding, cap at5,242,880bytes independently of headers, cancel/release the source reader on limit/abort/error/early response, and forward only the existing cookie/content-type/fixed Origin plus a validated Content-Length where appropriate. No Content-Encoding, arbitrary URL or new general proxy header is accepted. Preserve unrelated JSON/auth/report paths and response privacy unchanged. API independently enforces exact stored MIME and byte count, authenticates and checks current membership/READY/site/lease before pulling/buffering, reserves an admission permit, then reads bounded bytes outside a held DB transaction. Repeat authoritative gates/lease fences before decode, before the durable pre-PUT marker/dispatch and at final association.

Fixed local policies, not environment knobs: one active media binary pipeline per BFF process and one per API composition, no waiting queue,15s body-read budget,20s Sharp processing timeout,10s SDK write/read budget and45s caller wall budget within the existing60s processing lease. Busy requests fail before body buffering or processor admission. An API permit covers the real read/decode/native promise/SDK work and remains held until underlying work settles, even after caller abort/timeout; a Promise.race result is not native completion. Close stops admission, cancels body/SDK work and drains owned work before client/DB destruction. The installed Sharp buffer promise resolves from the native pipeline callback; keep that underlying promise as the completion owner. Apply timeout per pipeline and cooperate with AbortSignal between stages; do not claim destroy() or wall timeout immediately kills native work. No global sharp.cache/concurrency mutation, extra process/worker/service or unbounded retry is proposed. The input/output/pixel/concurrency limits bound admitted work but do not establish a hard RSS ceiling for native libvips buffers/cache. Verify the existing deployment memory/CPU limits and scoped real-decoder stress/RSS behavior before enablement; if inadequate, remain disabled and root arbitrates resource policy rather than inventing more app variables. Sharp's documented timer excludes time awaiting a libuv thread, so caller deadlines and drain ownership remain separate. [Sharp timeout](https://sharp.pixelplumbing.com/api-output/#timeout).

**Zero-schema candidate, explicit new semantics:** use stagingKey only as a never-uploaded server-generated inline-v1/site/asset/nonce marker for NEW leases; legacy staging/... leases are never adopted into this processor. Preserve every old row/key/ETag/URL/reference. The existing nullable text stableObjectVersion can store a versioned NEW-row tag with the expected input SHA256 at issuance and, only after server byte verification/decoding, the immutable input+derivative SHA256 pair. It is not an ETag or positive attestation while PENDING/PROCESSING. Persist the actual validated derivative MIME/size/dimensions and both hashes under the current claim before any PUT; public association still requires FINALIZED and its existing guards. Preserve original processingId, namespace and gifts/site/asset/processing.webp key in the durable publication intent. UNKNOWN/possible-PUT replay must never overwrite these hashes/metadata, change namespace/key or allocate another publication identity. A's schema/C1 owner must accept this tagged-field semantics explicitly; if not, root requests a separate minimal two-digest-field schema/test/migration proposal rather than silently relabeling legacy ETags. No schema or migration change is part of this B proposal.

**Same-lease concurrency/restart/retry:** claim/intent and byte binding use the existing locked transaction/CAS and exact microsecond timestamp helpers. Before any possible PUT, durable UNKNOWN commits and a fresh current lease/access/namespace fence must still pass. Publish immutable validated bytes to the one selected bucket with IfNoneMatch:"*". After timeout/abort/lost PUT or final-commit ACK, retain the original claim, tagged hashes, metadata and publication intent; do not clear its ownership as a generic FAILED row or declare expiry means no object. An expired active processor is fenced by its exact lease expiry; recovery may renew a lease on the ORIGINAL processing/key/namespace, so an older processor cannot associate or advance the newer lease. If no prior possible PUT exists, a proven pre-dispatch failure may retain ORPHANED and allow the reviewed same-byte retry while the upload lease is valid. Once possible PUT exists, future attempts consult/read/recover that original target. Conditional retransmission is allowed only by an explicit same-byte binary retry during the unexpired upload lease, with exact reencoded derivative hash matching the pinned original; changed bytes/codec output or unknown binding denies it. It creates no new key or overwrite. A412 is verified by bounded exact original readback, not treated as success by itself; a404 never authorizes a new identity or proves no late write. Restart loses transient original bytes, never retained database evidence.

Storage therefore needs derivative-only publish/readback and close ports, with captured bucketName/storageNamespace/publicOrigin. Reuse current bounded IfMatch HEAD/GET logic on derivative objects and verify actual read bytes against the pinned derivative SHA256/size/MIME; metadata/ETag alone cannot replace that check. Remote reads/writes obey abort/time budgets and sanitized errors. The service returns the stored canonical FINALIZED result without decoding or republishing on duplicate/recovery; positive readback plus fresh original-scope CAS may finish an interrupted association. Unknown readback remains retained, and no automatic deletion/reconciliation worker is added. Reconciliation/deletion must continue to reread actual FINALIZED/gift references and preserve every newer/associated object; this proposal never deletes an existing object or rewrites a historical URL.

**Current client/UI and UNKNOWN:** replace the raw presigned fetch with same-origin authenticated binary transport; apiRequest remains the JSON helper. Capture actual immutable panel owner, selected site/gift/capability and a synchronous attempt before hashing/lease/transport/callbacks. Fence every await/getter/form cleanup against owner/site/eligibility/instance/attempt replacement and abort owned transport on loss; an already dispatched publication may remain unknown. Persist only a versioned site/operator/gift/asset correlation marker before the binary POST, read it back, never overwrite another retained unknown marker, and compare exact marker before clearing. No File/raw bytes/hash/password/cookie/token enters durable browser storage. This marker is a recovery hint, not an atomic cross-tab mutex; backend claim/object fencing remains authoritative. Mount/reload with a marker performs consultation only, with no auto binary retry/RECOVER/PUT; explicit recovery and same-file re-selection drive their distinct guarded actions. Clear only positively correlated FINALIZED/known no-publication state after checking current catalog attachment; loss/expiry/404/unavailable does not silently start another upload. Lost catalog PATCH acknowledgment triggers a fresh catalog read, not automatic overwrite of a different image. Backend catalog transactions and publication eligibility remain unchanged. ## Team current-owner / instance / attempt protocol — accepted design, source candidate

Source: panelActor.ts exposes immutable getSnapshot/getServerSnapshot/subscribe/load/invalidate/reportFailure and runAuthentication. Its runAuthentication deliberately invalidates at INIT and settlement and prevents owner.load while native authentication is in flight. Existing login already separates those mandatory native-cookie coherence transitions from disposed/replaced UI callbacks. teamAcceptance.ts:acceptTeamInvitation takes an injected request adapter, signs in only for mode existing, then accepts the opaque invitation; new sends password only for server-authorized new-account creation, and signedIn sends token only. Backend siteTeam.ts rejects password for an existing account, requires matching authenticated invited account, and new success does not sign in. These existing helpers and rules remain frozen.

### Team manager

SiteTeamSection captures the actual verified immutable owner snapshot/epoch plus siteId/canManage and current active manageTeam membership; trusted platform OWNER may use its existing server-enforced operational team capability, not any financial authority. Unknown owner, false capability, foreign/non-managing selected membership or expired/invalid clock cannot dispatch. Subscribe to owner changes synchronously, dispose the current instance and clear private link/target/results on scope loss; never adopt the replacement snapshot inside an existing operation. An outer real-owner/scoped runtime key or equivalent scoped state fences prop replacement before old data/controls can render. SSR stays unverified and disabled.

Each effect instance owns one controller and monotonic request/attempt revision, with synchronous in-flight reservation before publishing pending. Entry/current refresh loads only the team's strict scoped GET. Mutation explicitly compares a fresh strict /v1/me with the captured actor/session/memberships before dispatch; default transports remain existing apiRequest/client helpers, with a guarded request adapter checking current instance/site/capability/owner/attempt immediately before every transport. Invite/revoke/refresh settlement checks after every await and every reentrant callback. Invite only constructs/copies a validated same-site private fragment link after successful current issuance; clipboard access, write dispatch, subsequent team GET, token/link publication, form reset/toast and pending cleanup all require the same live attempt. A clipboard write or server mutation already dispatched cannot be undone; obsolete completion cannot issue later work or overwrite/clear another clipboard. Current401/403 may call frozen owner.reportFailure with the captured epoch; stale errors never report against a newer owner. Errors show sanitized existing feedback, not raw secrets.

### Invitation acceptance and legitimate transitions

Entry is one explicit owner identity load/current strict MeResponse validation (401 establishes anonymous; denied/unavailable/malformed/future/expired identity does not). No polling, fake epoch or arbitrary scope adoption. Own entry reads may use the frozen observable owner/load lifecycle; a late entry settlement must be the current returned owner snapshot, while an invalidated/replaced request cannot install local identity. The original valid hash token stays only in the live component/controller's transient memory and the hash is removed after capture; no token in request URLs, storage, logs or generic public state. StrictMode setup creates a fresh controller from the same mounted private token holder; obsolete cleanup cannot dispose the replacement. A true unmount, success or invalidation discards that instance's password/token callbacks. Password input is cleared synchronously on submit/disposal; no password in state, toast, URL or storage.

Acceptance reserves one attempt synchronously; mode/token/form/context replacement makes earlier callbacks obsolete and cannot clear a newer pending state. Every helper transport is wrapped, so frozen acceptTeamInvitation's existing two-step sign-in/accept sequence cannot dispatch its second POST after scope loss. SignedIn requires the exact current verified owner/native session and a fresh strict matching MeResponse immediately before accept. Success is a server-restricted membership grant; only that still-current attempt may perform one explicitly owned synchronous cache invalidation and publish the current result. Anonymous new requires a current observed anonymous owner and fresh401 before POST, uses no runAuthentication/sign-in, never changes an existing password/account, and completes signedIn:false with explicit subsequent login; it does not need an old authenticated epoch or reset a different cached owner.

Existing-account sign-in is an explicit legitimate authentication transition, not a requirement to keep its former epoch forever. Wrap only this explicit existing flow with frozen owner.runAuthentication. A private transition observer allows exactly one synchronously attributable INIT notification and exactly one settlement notification from that owned wrapper; all unrelated owner notifications, extra/reentrant invalidations, new loading/verified snapshots, disposal or attempt replacement cancel local continuation. The wrapper's mandatory INIT/final invalidations are native-cookie coherence already established by the frozen login contract, not late component resetPanelActorCache callbacks. The component never adds a stale final cache reset. While the native sign-in resolves, the attempt retains its private owned unverified frame; after successful sign-in the guarded adapter obtains a fresh strict /v1/me, validates the expected normalized submitted account email and native identity/expiry, and binds subsequent accept/post-accept comparison to that exact subject/session. Password/token data is never decoded to infer identity. A changed cookie/identity is rejected by native fresh checks; matching ordinary email alone cannot authorize a different invitation, which remains server-bound.

Expected auth INIT/settlement events use finite single-event budgets around the known wrapper call and native-operation completion; if another event occurs in a budget window, the actual wrapper event makes the count exceed one and the attempt fails closed. Outside those two attributable windows, any owner notification cancels the operation. Do not call owner.load during the guarded native-auth phase or adopt an arbitrary new verified owner to resume it. Completion may use the current owned unverified frame created by the wrapper plus the same strict directly observed native session; opening the panel performs its existing fresh owner verification. Anonymous acceptance and signedIn membership invalidation have separate bounded semantics. No server-cookie response already sent can be rolled back by this UI; the caller fences future requests/UI and the frozen owner maintains native authentication coherence.

## Listening

The team callers now preserve their actual native owner, component instance and reserved attempt across asynchronous work. We selected explicit bounded runAuthentication transitions because an existing-account sign-in/sign-out legitimately changes cached authority; unconditional old-epoch equality would break those flows, while blindly adopting a new owner would allow stale continuation. New-account acceptance stays anonymous and the backend remains responsible for preserving existing passwords/accounts. Getter/callable checks and actual owner identity prevent a stale callback from resetting a newer view/cache or publishing/copying a private capability. We reused all frozen helpers and kept the source unit inside four paths; no global owner export was changed to claim detection of silent factory disposal. Actual authenticated/provider gates and the separately authorized read-only media proposal remain follow-up work owned by root.

## Withdrawal confirmation and recovery design — root21:54, REVIEWED LOCAL_READY

### Verified source boundary

The cheap Git-root/graph triage found the existing graph. A bounded read-only graph query for WithdrawalHistory/FinanceOverview/WithdrawalIntent/ReceivingSetup/financialReauthentication returned no matching nodes; direct source inspection supplied the evidence. No index update or global job ran. Existing FinanceSection.tsx independently loads overview and history with selected-site/access/revision ownership, but does not receive sessionEpoch. ReceivingSectionView already has the actual epoch and passes only access/site to FinanceSection. WithdrawalHistoryView displays amount, fee, net, timestamps and truthful states; it has no create/recovery control. FinancialOverview CURRENT means the server projected a balance observation, not recipient designation or authority to reserve/transfer.

C1 WithdrawalIntent/list have no requestId, verified masked destination, quote reference or operator designation. The currently edited A transport adds strict {requestId,amountCents,destinationVersion,reauthenticationToken} and success {requestId,withdrawal}, with unchanged historical intent/list shapes. These are provisional source observations, not a released contract or production endpoint. The new requestId envelope permits response correlation, but list/history cannot rediscover a lost operation by requestId. No preview/context/recovery-status consumer contract exists.

The existing receivingDestination.maskedDescriptor is nullable with a120-character database bound (packages/database/src/financialSchema.ts226-260). A can project that existing value only after verifying its exact original account/environment/bankReference/version association; presence of a string alone proves no bank validation. No new bank-data field or schema migration is assumed. GiftEditorView.tsx48-60 already exports parseGiftPriceToCents with exact decimal-to-safe-integer parsing; reuse that read-only utility and its established input convention rather than add a floating-point parser or new money dependency.

withdrawalReservations.ts retains a private scoped fee quote, verified receiving/account/recipient context, provider balance and locked holds. It obtains the fee during first reservation; the browser has never accepted that exact snapshot. Its request hash binds site/request/operator/environment/amount/destinationVersion, not session or rotating proof. Existing replay looks up the request, preserves the old account/bank/recipient/fee/balance/audit/hold and makes no fresh fee/balance call; it permits a new current session for the same operator and rejects another operator or changed amount/version. Both lookup and locked replay still require current session/membership/proof. Current context must remain structurally valid for the configured environment; no promise is made that recovery works when that service scope itself is unavailable. The actual repository has no accepted native transfer/completion writer: mocked replay of every C1 state does not establish PostgreSQL support for terminal writes.

Concrete post-commit ambiguity: withdrawalReservations.ts936-955 checks proof/fee/balance again AFTER reserveWithdrawal has committed. A403 RECENT_AUTH_REQUIRED,409 or503 response can therefore follow an actual HELD reservation. Once a reservation POST might have dispatched, no HTTP status alone proves that it did not reserve; the marker and original command must survive all such failures. Only exact authoritative recovery or a durable delayed-request-fencing seal changes that uncertainty.

The accepted ReceivingSetup guard captures immutable owner snapshot/epoch/current selected membership, compares strict direct /v1/me plus server context before proof installation and again before one-shot consumption, and ignores obsolete settlement. Its private proof quarantine and actual form disposal are reusable through FinancialReauthenticationGuard; the nomination-specific retained-context controller is not a withdrawal controller. REQUEST_WITHDRAWAL proof scope currently requires readFinance, not the requestWithdrawal boolean. The latter is useful UI metadata only; a future withdrawal caller additionally requires server-designated operator, verified destination and explicit new/recovery eligibility. No B change to existing authorization semantics is proposed implicitly.

### Smallest user flow and consent boundary

Keep the overview/history read-only. Add one withdrawal setup panel only after reviewed A dependencies and a separate root source grant. It first reads current protected context and unresolved-request discovery. Unknown/missing context, unverified owner, assessor/foreign site, undesignated recipient or unavailable verified destination cannot expose an enabled new action. Recovery discovery runs before new-action eligibility; changed/deactivated current receiving details must not hide an old request. The backend may permit a further explicitly new withdrawal after a known reservation, subject to current holds and funds; the UI does not require every older request to be terminal or infer eligibility from history.

For a genuinely new action, the user enters a BRL amount using the established money-input convention, interpreted as total reservation/debit including fee. Submit an authenticated amount-only preview request. Display the server's exact requested amount, fee and positive net (amount = fee + net), verified masked destination/beneficiary label, sandbox/live mode and quote expiry. Do not derive the fee from 2%, free allowances, gift commission, displayed aggregate balance or a browser estimate; no bank/provider value is invented. Use an explicit final confirmation such as “Confirmar solicitação de saque”, explaining that REQUESTED records a reservation and does not confirm transfer or bank credit. Reuse FinancialReauthenticationForm with fixed REQUEST_WITHDRAWAL purpose. Amount or accepted quote/destination/policy change clears confirmation and proof; expired/uncertain preview disables dispatch and requires a new displayed preview and explicit consent. No automatic submit follows preview/password success.

Client comparison alone cannot bind consent across the network/commit race. Before a NEW reservation, the server must authenticate the accepted immutable preview reference, bind it to the current subject/site/environment/request and full internal account/recipient/bank/version/amount/fee/net/policy/expiry snapshot, and compare it under the existing reservation lock. If current destination/recipient/fee policy or quote validity differs, reject before any new hold and ask the user to review again. Balance/event/hold drift is revalidated under the existing lock; insufficient or unknown funds rejects rather than adjusting amount/net. A fresh fee response must match the accepted snapshot exactly or reject; it cannot silently replace the accepted price. Server proof is purpose/session confirmation only, not this consent snapshot and not native transfer authority.

### Required A handoff; illustrative exact candidate, NOT executable C1

Root/A must arbitrate a separately granted context/preview/recovery/consent unit after the current eight-file transport release. The current four-field create wire cannot express preview acceptance or recovery-only intent; B will not consume it for an enabled new action and will not edit A's files. Recommended small protected service methods and strict public projections are:

- readContext({actor,access}): {siteId,mode,observedAt,newRequestAllowed,reason,recipient:null|{isCurrentOperator:true,displayName},destination:null|{version,verified:true,maskedLabel},unresolvedRequests:[{requestId,amountCents,destinationVersion}]}. Every allowed value is server-derived. True newRequestAllowed requires independently valid stored designation/readiness/destination and configured service, but remains a preview affordance, not final authority. Discover retained dispatch/commit uncertainty across sessions/tabs even when no local marker exists. This MUST include issued OPEN/unsealed request/preview bindings without a withdrawal row, not merely persisted intents/UNKNOWN: a delayed first NEW may still commit after an apparently empty list. A must fence scope-level new-request issuance/eligibility, request confirmation and sealing atomically under an appropriate common server serialization boundary; a different requestId cannot bypass an unresolved prior OPEN binding. An abandoned pre-dispatch preview needs an explicit safe retirement/seal rule rather than silently disappearing. Missing/ambiguous history is unavailable, not an empty array. Exact reason enum and safe display fields require A review; no account/wallet/bank reference, document, credentials or audit hashes are public.
- preview({actor,access,amountCents}): {siteId,mode,requestId,quoteId,currency:"BRL",amountCents,feeCents,netCents,destinationVersion,maskedDestination,recipientDisplayName,policyVersion,observedAt,expiresAt}. The server assigns a fresh request/quote reference for this explicitly new preview lifecycle, retains an immutable internal binding and publishes a strict public projection. It creates no withdrawal, hold, operation or transfer. Quote reference is non-secret correlation and confers no standalone authority. Clock, expiry, account-scoped fee provenance, verified destination masking and conservation are validated by A; zero fee is allowed only when an actual verified quote says zero. Masked historical presentation must be recoverable from the original verified destination binding, not reconstructed from the current account. Preview storage/signature/discovery mechanics and their minimal A paths are A/root decisions, not a B migration grant.
- readRecovery({actor,access,requestId}): every state has the strict common envelope {siteId,requestId,mode,state,observedAt}, including UNRESOLVED when no original command can be recovered. The exact request-bound public union adds EXISTING {original command,original masked preview,withdrawal}, UNRESOLVED {original command when known}, or SEALED_NOT_CREATED {original command,sealReference}. It must find the stored original operator/site/environment/request and immutable destination/fee association. An absent row,404, empty list, expired quote or failed read means unresolved; only a committed seal that fences delayed NEW reservation under the same request lock proves no reservation can later appear. Current read authorization governs disclosure; historical masking unavailable is reported as unavailable without substituting new bank details. Seal reference is non-secret evidence reference, not a client assertion or financial release capability. B validates the exact site/request/environment and server observation ordering in every branch before changing retained uncertainty.
- Confirm/recover action on the existing withdrawal path: propose a strict discriminated wire with NEW {action:"CONFIRM_NEW",requestId,amountCents,destinationVersion,quoteId,reauthenticationToken} versus recovery-only {action:"RECOVER_EXISTING",requestId,amountCents,destinationVersion,reauthenticationToken}. This is an explicit future breaking C1 amendment, not a change to A's granted wire. NEW requires accepted preview equality/expiry and atomic context/fund fences; recovery-only must NEVER fall through to first creation when lookup is missing. Exact replay returns the original snapshot/state without requote/new hold/dispatch and uses fresh current proof. Do not put proof/quote in the unchanged withdrawal.reservation.v1 historical identity or rewrite existing records; preview consent requires a separately retained association that A validates against that same request. Response remains strictly correlated {requestId,withdrawal}; validate original amount/version/site/mode, and NEW amount/fee/net against the accepted preview before displaying acknowledgment. A response mismatch after possible dispatch remains unknown, even if backend conservation alone passes.

Candidate routes are GET /v1/sites/:siteId/withdrawals/context, POST /v1/sites/:siteId/withdrawals/preview, GET /v1/sites/:siteId/withdrawals/:requestId and the amended POST /v1/sites/:siteId/withdrawals. These names/methods are proposed only; A must publish accepted C1 before implementation. Preview POST is a protected quote request, not a reservation or provider transfer. GET discovery/recovery never reserves, seals, rotates an identity or dispatches. If sealing is needed, A must expose an explicitly invoked protected command rather than hide a write in GET. All endpoints strictly validate path/body, authenticate selected membership, use no-store/sanitized errors, and keep private provider references server-side. A must define freshness, expiry, authoritative observation ordering and cross-tab request-fencing semantics. Default ports remain absent; no startup enablement follows.

### Identity, reload and uncertainty rules

One explicit NEW preview lifecycle gets one fresh server requestId; it is NOT derived only from user/site/amount/destination or sessionEpoch. A later legitimate same-amount operation gets a distinct identity only after an explicit new action and authoritative server eligibility. Repeated confirm/click/timeouts for the same lifecycle retain the original request/quote/amount/destination; no remount, reload, session replacement, quote refresh or transport error creates another operation. A transient invalid preview before dispatch may be abandoned; once dispatch is possible, refresh must not overwrite the retained command or choose a replacement identity.

Before any reservation POST, persist and verify only a versioned non-secret dispatch marker {siteId,operatorUserId,mode,requestId,quoteId,amountCents,destinationVersion,phase:"DISPATCH_POSSIBLE"}. No password, proof, sessionId/epoch, membership/capability, masked bank/beneficiary details, fee authority or credentials enter browser storage/logs/URLs. The marker is correlation only and never grants permission. If storage is denied, corrupted, replaced or cannot be read back, do not dispatch a new operation unless a reviewed server durable-discovery/intent receipt already covers that precise request; initially choose the blocked path. A missing/corrupt local marker never proves no earlier action: fresh protected server discovery is mandatory. Distinct-user markers stay quarantined and cannot disclose old details to the replacement actor; never clear another user's uncertainty to enable a new action. Existing-marker comparison before update avoids overwriting another tab's request; browser locking is not the authoritative serialization boundary.

On possible POST dispatch, timeout, late rejection, malformed/foreign response, controller disposal, logout or server commit-ack uncertainty, preserve the dispatch marker and unknown outcome. Purge transient password/proof/consent/controller authority immediately. Entry performs only current context and, when needed, exact request recovery reads; subsequent recovery is manually requested. There is no automatic replay/POST/reauthentication or background keepalive. A new current owner/session may recover the same non-secret marker after fresh strict server identity/access comparison. An existing request keeps its original amount, fee/net, account/destination/version and held commitment even when current receiving readiness/designation changed; current membership/proof protects action recovery, and no new READY/current-bank gate rewrites history. Another operator can read only what A authorizes and cannot action-replay the original operator's command.

EXISTING switches from “result unknown” to the authoritative original operation status. REQUESTED means held reservation, PROCESSING is provider progress, COMPLETED remains provider completion without claiming bank credit. UNKNOWN never releases holds or enables replacement. Keep durable request correlation after known reservation so later uncertainty/reload can still find it; replace/retire only through an exact server-backed history/archive rule, never by amount matching. SEALED_NOT_CREATED may retire that exact uncertain marker only after the delayed-request fence is proven; then a separately confirmed new preview may use a new request. Explicit recovery POST requires freshly confirmed REQUEST_WITHDRAWAL proof and recovery-only dispatch, so a missing historical request cannot unexpectedly reserve funds. Original quote expiry/new policy may block NEW but must not prevent authenticated read/existing-only recovery of old commitments. Cross-tab NEW confirmation must reject if server discovery/locked fences reveal unresolved earlier dispatch; two independent known legitimate operations remain distinguishable and subject to authoritative held balance.

### Minimal proposed B paths; no source lease granted

Eight exact paths are sufficient, with one B writer per file: NEW apps/admin/src/components/withdrawalClient.ts and withdrawalClient.test.ts; NEW apps/admin/src/components/WithdrawalSetup.tsx and WithdrawalSetup.test.ts; EXISTING apps/admin/src/components/FinanceSection.tsx and FinanceSection.test.ts; EXISTING apps/admin/src/components/ReceivingSection.tsx and ReceivingSection.test.ts. The client owns strict C1 transport/parsing, operation-state/correlation and injected storage functions; Setup owns the current-owner/epoch guard, UI and effect lifecycle. This avoids a separate generic money framework, duplicate finance card/history or extra persistence package. Reuse the existing parseGiftPriceToCents utility without changing its file; no dependency is needed.

ReceivingSection passes its existing epoch to FinanceSection; FinanceSection conditionally composes WithdrawalSetup while preserving independent existing reads. Reuse FinancialReauthenticationForm/Guard and apiRequest unchanged, with injected synthetic transport/owner/clock/storage for source tests. Existing ReceivingSetup, password/proof/session owner, Panel/SiteWorkspace/login, A's cards, WithdrawalHistoryView/withdrawalHistory, shared schemas/API/runtime/public/catalog/media/manifests remain frozen unless root later names a demonstrated additional path. Extend the client wire only through A-exported shared schemas; do not duplicate a proposed schema locally while waiting. No mock READY, provider account choice or fabricated destination appears in production defaults.

### Order and finite acceptance gates

1. Publish this design and independent read-only review; root arbitrates A's preview/consent/recovery C1 and separately grants exact A/B source paths. A's current transport can finish unchanged; it does not unblock new-withdrawal UI. Any required consent persistence/repository/seal scope is requested explicitly, not added to the current eight-file unit.
2. After A publishes reviewed strict C1 and deterministic server-fencing evidence, B performs focused TDD for the client/controller and then mounts Setup within its eight proposed paths. Synthetic transport/owner/clock/storage are the only injected authority. Confirm RED before logic, then relevant source regressions/admin TypeScript/scoped Biome and independent review; do not add dependencies/global configuration.
3. Deterministic cases: exact cents/conservation/private-field rejection; foreign site/mode/request/quote; designation/destination absent; preview expiry/clock rollback; amount/policy/destination change resets consent/proof; fee drift rejected by A before hold; fresh Me/owner/epoch comparison and stale401 isolation; quarantine/expiry/one-shot consumption; reentrant disposal before/during validation and transport; storage failure/readback mismatch; same-request late/lost ACK/UNKNOWN; reload/new session same operator; different actor quarantined; missing/404 remains unknown; sealed absence fences delayed NEW; old destination/account/quote expiry recovery; two later same-amount new actions have distinct request IDs; concurrent tabs cannot overwrite uncertain prior intent or double-hold one request. - The existing strict MeResponse already contains session.id and expiresAt, plus user and memberships: packages/contracts/src/index.ts:463-478. - Panel loads once per mount (apps/admin/src/components/Panel.tsx:47-73), snapshots selected membership (109-139), and keys SiteWorkspace/receiving only by site (Panel.tsx:134-139; SiteWorkspace.tsx:445-446; routes/sites.$siteId.receiving.tsx:7-9). ### Smallest proposed seams; not released implementation

1. **B session ownership:** extend existing panelActor.ts/test with strict meResponseSchema parsing and one observable generation/snapshot owner; Panel.tsx subscribes and passes a nullable verified sessionEpoch through SiteWorkspace.tsx to ReceivingSection.tsx. Identity is derived from validated user.id/session.id plus selected membership/capabilities, not a constant, Date.now or a hash of browser credentials. On logout initiation, auth/identity uncertainty, expired/denied session, cache reset or new refetch generation, publish unverified/null epoch immediately, dispose proof/action controllers and ignore earlier request settlement. Publish a fresh epoch only after current-generation validation; same user/site in a new session still changes generation. Emit only tokenless invalidation hints across tabs; hints revoke local confidence but never establish permission. Revalidate on entry and explicit sensitive intent/return to an active tab, without background keepalive polling. Remote revocation is not instantly observable without server push; fail closed at observed invalidation and require authoritative endpoint checks on every action.
2. **B/A proof-context decision:** the current password response contains only token/expiry, and the keyed form trusts its supplied epoch. A cookie may change in another tab during the POST. The reviewed caller must quarantine a returned proof until current validated session/access still matches the captured generation. Prefer a fresh /v1/me identity verification before proof installation, dropping proof and invalidating on mismatch/failure, while separating this validation from a blind epoch-reset loop that would invalidate every newly issued proof. This requires a reviewed composition/request-adapter seam in FinancialReauthenticationForm.tsx and financialReauthenticationClient.ts/test; do not parse an opaque proof token or infer its subject. An alternative server response-context amendment is a root/A contract decision, not necessary evidence already present. The action's existing server proof fence remains authoritative for races after the check.
3. **A context and stable operation:** propose one authenticated, no-store GET receiving/nomination-context in new receivingNominationContext.ts/test and receivingNominationHttp.ts/test, with a root-approved strict C1 addition in packages/contracts/src/internalGifts.ts/index.ts and app.ts registration. It must read the same configured intent policy and service availability, and discover the current canonical local nomination for this selected operator/site/environment, excluding provider/account/audit credentials. For no existing nomination, publish a stable non-secret operation requestId selected by the server for that immutable scope; concurrent tabs/refetches must get the same identity without depending on sessionEpoch. Select the exact server derivation/retention rule with A/root; GET must not create an account, reservation or identity. For an existing nomination, return its original requestId and exact pending projection rather than minting another ID. Current repository needs a scoped discovery reader in receivingNominationRepository.ts/test; do not expose its private snapshot. Browser storage/locks are optional coordination aids, not operation authority; server-canonical identity is preferred over a new browser persistence subsystem.
4. **Policy change/activation:** an uncertain or existing request must not silently receive a new policy or ID. Current service requires configured policy before replay and includes it in requestHash; pending old-policy replay therefore needs an explicit A/root rule. Minimal safe behavior is to preserve/show the canonical pending intent and block a changed-policy POST until that rule is released. A/root owns policy/configuration and optional service/signing/repository composition in receivingNomination.ts/test, receivingNominationRepository.ts/test, financialReauthentication.ts plus existing limiter/verifier, runtimeConfig.ts/test, index.ts and app.ts/test. No Asaas resource or READY activation is implied. B then loads the reviewed context in proposed receivingNominationContextClient.ts/test and composes ReceivingSection.tsx plus current ReceivingNominationForm.tsx/test, preserving A-owned feature cards. Mounting waits for explicit root scope release and the reviewed session/context/action seams.

Recommend the existing MeResponse-derived session owner plus one server context/read seam; retain the unmounted consumer until these are reviewed. Root/A must publish the exact context DTO/path, canonical operation-ID/discovery rule, pending-policy-change rule, and unavailable/default startup behavior before B composition. ## Listening

The finite remaining-release audit distinguishes completed source from missing operational behavior and unverified acceptance. Team callback ownership is a local safety gap that can be closed without provider access; saved image preview and finance paging/history need narrow reviewed read contracts rather than a new generic dashboard. Native recipient/bank/payout/reversal and runtime composition are missing implementation, while actual resources, identities, target migrations and real-money pilot remain separate authorization gates. The source-backed clarification about terminal totals avoids treating an isolated row parser or constructed fixture as complete financial aggregation. No source was reopened or executable test/browser/provider step taken during the audit; root can arbitrate the smallest next local unit without pretending the release is complete or entirely blocked on automated support.

The withdrawal implementation consumes the reviewed one-request C1 rather than duplicating the earlier illustrative quote DTO. Initial discovery is GET-only; every explicit preview/action uses the immutable current owner and direct strict Me/context comparison, while existing password proof quarantine remains unchanged. Minimal non-secret storage is verified before possible dispatch and cannot replace another request or actor. Exact BigInt revisions and stored microseconds order authoritative recovery; only strictly newer correlated knowledge can resolve UNRESOLVED, while stronger known reservation/seal/acknowledgment history survives later uncertainty. The storage callback audit also fences current ownership after final reads/removal and before publishing failures, preventing synchronous getter/setter side effects from restoring obsolete UI state. LocalStorage cannot provide a cross-tab atomic mutex, so backend common locking/consent/seal fencing remains a separate acceptance gate. Deadline timers only retract local proof/consent and never poll the server. Genuine DOM tests strengthen lifecycle evidence; real protected browser, PostgreSQL, provider fees/destination and bank receipt remain pending.

The withdrawal design separates accepted financial consent from password confirmation and separates an explicitly new operation from historical recovery. Reusing the existing exact cents parser, private proof guard and stored masked descriptor avoids a duplicate finance model or new bank-data collection. An immutable server preview checked under the reservation lock was selected because comparing fee/net after POST discovers a mismatch after the hold already exists. Request identity is fresh for each explicitly new lifecycle rather than deterministic by amount; correlation survives possible dispatch while proof/session authority does not. Recovery-only semantics and a durable delayed-request fence were selected over blindly retrying the create endpoint or treating404/403 as no commitment. Existing A transport remains its current eight-file source unit; the future consent/context/discovery handoff requires separate root arbitration before any B control is mounted. Actual PostgreSQL/provider/bank receipt and protected browser acceptance remain separate follow-ups.

Receiving composition now mounts the existing forms through current server context rather than treating a role flag, policy string or locally retained operation as permission. The same immutable owner snapshot and actual generation remain authoritative for UI lifetime; direct Me/context comparisons avoid a global refresh/remount loop and idle-extending background polling. A password result is quarantined privately until comparison and deadline checks pass, then nomination repeats comparison before one-shot consumption. Controller-owned lifetime checks close dispatch after child disposal while the surrounding guard stays current. A manual read-only recovery action preserves uncertainty and original server binding; it never automatically posts, and replay requires a newly confirmed proof. PENDING is always read-only and truthful about outstanding provider verification. jsdom was added only by the authorized manifest owner in a serialized window; actual DOM effects improve source evidence without claiming a real protected browser or provider flow. Default startup/receiving/catalog/provider gates remain intact.

Observable session ownership now retracts every actor/epoch on refresh, uncertainty, authorization denial, authentication INIT, local suspension and tokenless cross-tab invalidation. Only a strict fresh current-generation MeResponse installs immutable authority; reentrant subscribers and immediate deadline expiry cannot receive an obsolete successful actor. Entry, deliberate verification and resume read /me; one cancellable expiry timer avoids background reads that would extend idle activity. SSR exposes a stable unverified snapshot. Per-effect authentication attempt disposal separately fences late navigation/toasts because authentication intentionally invalidates the session epoch. The role/navigation contracts and unmounted financial forms remain intact. The backend read path uses a separate native auth factory rather than mutating the shared default or globally changing cleanup; SDK-created cookie-expiry instructions are distinguished from headers actually forwarded. Protected mounted proof ownership, startup/context composition and actual browser/database acceptance remain separate authorized follow-ups.

The mounting audit reuses the existing server session identity instead of inventing a session credential or a constant epoch. A local epoch only owns asynchronous UI/proof lifetime; the server remains responsible for current membership, session, proof and locked nomination authorization. Server policy and canonical operation discovery are proposed together so retries, remounts and concurrent tabs cannot silently select new IDs or policies. A browser-only storage scheme was rejected as the primary authority, and automatic /me polling was rejected because it extends the existing idle session. The finite source audit changes only this status; actual seams, policy-change semantics and activation require the explicit root/A handoff.

This browser amendment approves the observed fictional history/recovery behavior and measured geometry only. Normal reload provides a real remount without pretending that the unsupported native close gesture passed. The debug counter resets per document, so retained-operation checks and absence of later attempts carry the recovery claim; the late receipt comparison baseline is stated rather than enlarged into complete initial identity proof. The catalog observation follows the previously approved unknown-eligibility hide contract, avoiding an invented error surface. Capture, actual mobile scrolling, cancellation, protected/template/provider acceptance remain explicit follow-ups. No source changes were made during the frozen run.

Nomination is an explicit self-indication with an authoritative pending response, not a claim of provider account creation or permission to receive funds. The existing purpose-bound password proof is consumed only after the selected-site/session/policy and explicit confirmation gates. The consumer never invents an operation ID or policy version: the future reviewed caller owns that identity across retries and session replacement, which avoids replacing an uncertain reservation with a second operation. Raw failures and malformed/foreign responses remain uncertain without exposing secrets; a later early rejection cannot erase prior uncertainty. Detected loss of scope permanently invalidates its controller, even if the former values return before an old response. Persistent secrets, automatic retries, legal/provider consent claims and premature mounting were excluded. Protected mounted QA and the executable session/policy/action seam remain follow-ups outside this source-only unit.

The real TEST failures exposed a mock boundary mismatch rather than permission to relax the service. Catalog and media normalize stored SQL timestamps through A's shared strict decoder; injected clocks keep their Date-only guards. Native array binding replaces SQL tuple interpolation while keeping the site filter and immutable media projection. Media comparisons preserve stored microseconds for active leases and exact claim identity, because millisecond truncation could otherwise permit premature takeover or accept a changed claim expiry. Existing deterministic fixtures now project raw strings on SELECT by default and also cover native Dates. Global parser changes and contract changes were rejected; central owns the same31 PostgreSQL rerun after both reviewed source freezes.

The recovery correction separates current confidence from historical financial outcome. A's authenticated unresolved evidence can replace the current UI receipt/refund claim with verification, while its prior terminal history remains a permanent no-new-charge floor. Paired existing C1 status snapshots preserve both facts across remount and tied timestamps; numeric instant comparison treats whole-second and millisecond UTC equally. Storage updates compare the complete current marker/history before writing, and clearing refuses any stored terminal floor, including one written by a concurrent consultation. This preserves ledger independence and the existing CLOSED_UNPAID release for operations without such history. Fresh authoritative proof must be newer than recorded uncertainty to restore certainty; no browser/financial reconciliation acceptance follows from reducer tests.

## Saved/draft image preview — finite read-only proposal, 2026-10-13 05:54

State: LOCAL_READY / PROPOSAL_ONLY. The read-only assessment is complete; implementation waits for a separate exact source grant. Only this status document was edited. All eighteen accepted media files remain frozen.

### Decision and verified source boundary

Reuse the existing protected `POST /v1/sites/:siteId/gift-media/:assetId/finalize` with exactly JSON `{}` for the asset referenced by a freshly read administrative catalog gift. No new API route, public projection, shared contract, recovery marker or client recovery-controller change is required for current inline assets.

Verified source: `giftMediaHttp.ts` routes JSON{} to `consultGiftMediaUpload`, then strictly parses and correlates the status site/asset. Its same-origin administrative authentication and read-only native-session revalidation remain authoritative. `giftMedia.ts` consultation only reads the asset and publication intents inside the existing tracked, locked authorization scope; it does not claim, associate, upload or recover. That scope requires configured storage/namespace/processing, the same native actor/session, fresh site/manageCatalog/READY checks and lifecycle eligibility. Configuration/readiness absence continues to deny access.

The service returns media only for FINALIZED with a valid `inline-v1:verified` binding and exactly one matching current-namespace ASSOCIATED publication intent. Original upload expiry alone does not invalidate this finalized projection. `publicMedia` validates the stored HTTPS URL through C1, rejects credentials/query/fragment, requires its decoded pathname to match the pinned derivative key, and enforces WebP/size/dimension limits. Return that validated stored URL unchanged; never construct one from a storage key, bucket or current host configuration.

Legacy, partial or contradictory tuples remain UNKNOWN with no media. The new path must not relabel those rows, infer publication from a public catalog response, accept `canRetrySameBytes` as preview permission, or adopt an image by inspecting a raw key. Existing public catalog lookup is unsuitable for DRAFT and uses a different historical projection.

The current editor already owns the actual immutable panelActorOwner snapshot, native session epoch, site/manageCatalog selection, captured request/transport/time identities, lifetime abort and catalog revisions. It already imports the authenticated request transport and passes an optional public asset to GiftImageField. The missing operation is a saved-asset consultation during catalog loading; createGiftMediaController.start/consult currently require an actual retained upload marker, and its run/json paths are coupled to marker storage/recovery state. Reusing those methods would require unnecessary changes to frozen recovery behavior.

### Exact smallest proposed source grant

No edits are proposed to giftMediaClient.ts/.test.ts, GiftImageField.tsx/.test.ts, GiftEditorView, giftCatalogClient, API/service/storage/proxy/app/auth/composition, C1, database or A's receiving9. GiftImageField already renders the matching asset/site URL with the current gift description and shows the existing saved-image text when no projection exists. If root requires historical assets without the current proof or atomic gift-plus-media reads, that is a separate contract/source decision; this proposal does not weaken consultation to satisfy it.

### Read flow, correlation and lifecycle

1. After the existing authenticated catalog read, capture the exact accepted gift/site/asset/altText/publicationState/updatedAt and current editor generation. Only non-null saved mediaAssetId with a valid nonempty current description is a preview candidate; support DRAFT and PUBLISHED without a publish gate. Archived cards can use the same protected proof if still displayed by the existing editor. Do not interpret a locally selected file or marker asset as the saved catalog attachment.
2. Run one finite read pass for that accepted catalog generation. Deduplicate saved asset IDs within the pass and serialize consultations, with one active native request at a time. Send only authenticated JSON{} to the established encoded mediaFinalize path; strictly parse the full response and require FINALIZED plus matching status and media site/asset. The ordinary catalog remains usable with the existing saved-image placeholder while preview reads are pending; preview work does not take upload/recovery business ownership.
3. After positive media responses, perform one fresh strict administrative catalog read for the pass before publishing any thumbnails. Compare each exact captured row association and revision against that fresh result and the live accepted selection. A removed/replaced asset, deleted gift, changed description/publication/updatedAt, cross-site row or invalid catalog prevents that preview. A conflicting fresh row is masked and requires the existing explicit catalog reload; do not automatically PATCH, adopt the new association or launch an unbounded reread loop. Do not call marker confirmCatalog/start or change recovery state merely to validate a preview.
4. Store only ephemeral, correlated preview projections. A new catalog load, accepted replacement, mutation/media action, session/owner/site/port replacement, loss of eligibility or disposal synchronously invalidates the affected projection/generation before external callbacks. getState and the mounted render must consult the authoritative live projection rather than retaining an old view.state URL. Repeat the final private active/revision/association checks after external owner/clock/isCurrent/callback getters, before dispatch and before committing/notifying.
5. Give the saved-read request the same bounded 45-second child abort linked to the editor lifetime. Retain ownership until the actual request settles; an abort/deadline must not release a pending native promise through Promise.race. Supersession aborts and masks the old pass immediately; a replacement preview pass may dispatch only after the actual previous preview request settles and its newly captured generation remains current. Dispose ends work; no polling, automatic recovery/retry, new asset identity, upload/hash/file read, marker write/remove or background publication follows.
6. Keep invalid/UNKNOWN/PENDING/PROCESSING/FAILED/EXPIRED/404/503 projections empty and preserve the saved association text. Preserve actionable existing catalog/session/eligibility errors: current 401/403/session and readiness/lifecycle failures use existing guarded invalidation/reporting; stale callbacks cannot invalidate a replacement owner. A transient preview failure is not a receiving-enablement decision or a new upload error, and must not overwrite retained-upload uncertainty.

Cost: one consultation per distinct saved asset plus one shared attachment-confirmation catalog read for positive candidates, per explicit accepted refresh; no provider SDK read occurs in the FINALIZED projection branch. This adds protected HTTP/database read work and existing transaction locks. It is a bounded client pass, not a bulk API or performance acceptance claim.

Freshness limit: consultation and catalog confirmation are separate transactions. The result proves the last confirmed attachment snapshot plus current local/native-owner generation; it cannot prove that another operator never changes the database after that read. Remote mutations are discovered on the next explicit refresh. updatedAt is an existing correlation field, not a new server compare-and-swap token. No atomicity claim, remote watch or polling is part of this proposal.

### Deterministic test plan — not executed

| Case | Required assertion |
| --- | --- |
| Saved DRAFT/PUBLISHED, no marker | Fresh administrative list -> one exact POST JSON{} for the stored asset -> fresh matching list -> image src equals the unchanged validated URL and alt equals the stored description. Original expired upload timestamp still permits a strict FINALIZED result. |
| Missing/uncertain projection | Null asset, empty description, malformed/extra-field response, wrong site/asset, unsafe URL, wrong media shape or any non-FINALIZED status cannot render an image or authorize retry. Legacy UNKNOWN remains UNKNOWN. |
| Fresh attachment conflict | Deferred media result followed by replaced/removed asset, missing gift, changed description/publication/updatedAt, invalid/cross-site/duplicate catalog rows or failed confirmation produces no old image and no PATCH/automatic reread. |
| Scope supersession | Owner snapshot/epoch/user/site/capability expiry, receiving invalidation, same-object ports.owner/request/transport/clock replacement, reload, mutation and dispose before/after either await synchronously mask getState/render and prevent stale dispatch, projection, reports or callbacks. |
| Reentrant getters/callbacks | Native owner/clock/isCurrent/notification/eligibility/reportFailure getter disposes or replaces scope during the final check; neither network dispatch nor state commit/reporter invocation can follow the lost scope. |
| Actual deadline/drain | Fake time aborts the linked child at 45 seconds; a controlled native promise remains pending until explicitly settled; no replacement preview dispatch occurs early, even if the old response eventually succeeds. Lifetime disposal also reaches the actual default fetch signal. |
| Error classification | Current native 401/403/readiness/lifecycle errors invalidate only the current owner through existing guards. 404/503/malformed media keep the association placeholder without setting recovery permission or mutating retained state. |
| Deduplication and termination | Multiple gifts sharing an asset produce one consultation and one final catalog read; distinct assets are serialized. A failed candidate does not publish stale media or start polling/recovery. No positive candidates means no additional confirmation read. |
| Coexistence with recovery | Existing retained marker/blocked storage stays unchanged, including a retained new asset different from the saved attachment. Preview does not call createGiftMediaController upload/consult/recover/confirmCatalog or manufacture/clear a marker. Existing explicit recovery/attachment/caption regressions remain green. |
| Mounted DOM | Actual GiftEditor + GiftImageField displays the saved image after reload from a fresh instance, preserves the placeholder while pending/uncertain, and removes src synchronously on invalidated generation/owner/attachment. No public/DRAFT fetch, automatic file selection or provider image-resource test is implied. |

Use real native owner fixtures, strict C1 data, deferred authenticated JSON promises, fake timers and the existing jsdom mounted editor. Inspect the complete request log: only existing administrative catalog GET and exact mediaFinalize POST{} are allowed by the preview pass; lease, RECOVER, binary transport, catalog writes, storage mutation, hashing and generated identity calls remain absent.

### Recommendation and exact proposed paths

Add one deliberate “Carregar mais solicitações” read to the existing withdrawal setup, accumulating confirmed existing request references in private discovery state. Keep the complete uncursored canonical context and financial selection/proof lifecycle separate. One click consumes at most one previously issued cursor; no automatic loop or page queue.

Existing canonical/observation comparators and financial/marker methods remain semantically unchanged. |

Native scope/port checks and projection masking must also cover mounted in-place replacement. |

### Verified contract and compatibility boundary

The existing protected `GET /v1/sites/:siteId/withdrawals/context?cursor=...` accepts exactly one cursor parameter. C1 requires a nonempty 1–256-character opaque string from `[A-Za-z0-9._:-]`, or null for response exhaustion, and at most100 recoverable rows. Invalid/duplicate parameters are rejected by financeHttp; the response is strict, site-correlated and no-store. The service passes the validated cursor to its existing reader.

Current repository source filters associations/native facts to the authenticated site/operator/environment, revalidates authority in a repeatable-read read-only transaction, aggregates whole-scope obligations before pagination, orders request IDs lexically ascending, excludes the current OPEN from the recoverable page, and emits a keyset cursor from the last row. Its cursor implementation is not an operator/revision snapshot token. B must treat it as opaque: no prefix parsing, ID extraction, construction, credential/storage persistence or ownership inference from its text.

C1 correlates site/mode/scope revision throughout the context and nested requests, validates exact request/record observation dimensions and immutable terms, and forbids duplicate IDs within a page. Revision and microsecond strings are canonical nonnegative decimal strings; use the existing schemas/BigInt comparator behavior, never floating-point revision arithmetic or local timestamp truncation.

The existing compareWithdrawalContexts deliberately considers a changed page list or nextCursor at the same scopeRevision incomparable. Tests explicitly enforce that. Feeding the next page to installContext, normalizing/fabricating a replacement context to make that comparator pass, or relaxing the comparator would violate the existing financial authority boundary.

Use the frozen full comparator only between uncursored canonical reads. Validate an additional page independently as discovery: matching site/mode/exact scopeRevision and observationOrder, matching whole-scope reason/newRequestAllowed, unchanged current OPEN identity/immutable terms through compareWithdrawalObservations, and nonregressive/nonfuture observedAt. Those checks allow the expected page-list/cursor difference without installing a page as state.context. Rows still pass C1 and the unchanged per-ID observation comparator.

### Finite interaction and state preservation

1. Seed discovery from the explicitly accepted first canonical context. Bind its exact issued nextCursor to the actual verified owner object/snapshot, operator/session/epoch, selected site/access/mode and canonical/financial generation. Capture request/time/storage port identities; resample native scope and finish with private generation/active checks after external getters/callbacks. Cursors from another generation or an arbitrary caller cannot dispatch.
2. A deliberate next-page click uses protected GET only. Coalesce/ignore repeated clicks while one page operation is pending; do not queue future pages. Use read-only native `/v1/me` comparison against the captured actor/session before consuming the cursor, consistent with the existing financial guard's native comparison without forcing owner.load or requesting a proof. Read one cursor page, then one fresh uncursored canonical context for confirmation. Recheck ownership after every settlement and before reports/projection.
3. Require the fresh canonical head to remain logically “same” under the existing full comparator, with current clocks, and require the page header/OPEN observation to match that confirmed anchor. Changed revision, environment, reason/eligibility, immutable OPEN terms, older/future observation or incomparable data aborts this page and asks for an explicit current-context refresh. Do not install new authority or automatically restart/follow a new cursor. Separate GETs establish a confirmed snapshot, not atomic history.
4. Validate the entire batch before adding anything: unique IDs, strictly ascending lexical request-ID order within the page and beyond the last accepted boundary, matching original command/immutable terms, and only same/newer compatible observations against retained per-ID floors. Repeated/regressive/contradictory IDs or a reused/cyclic nextCursor are a page error, not an opportunity to skip or adopt rows. Empty-plus-continuation is no progress and cannot advance; empty-plus-null ends only this cursor chain, not proof of no obligation.
5. Append only fully accepted rows, retain per-ID observations including UNRESOLVED/EXISTING UNKNOWN and known terminal identity floors, and record successful cursor progress only after the full validation. Accumulation is ephemeral for the current owner/controller and grows only on explicit finite user requests. A canonical restart resets displayed page/cursor navigation while preserving same-owner request floors; owner replacement/disposal masks and releases private history. Missing rows, errors or old pages never erase a floor.
6. Keep a page-local loading/error/retry result. Ordinary unavailable/malformed/404/503 responses retain the last confirmed history and original request correlation, do not render an empty-success list, and allow only explicit same-cursor retry while its anchor remains valid. Current401/403/native authorization loss uses existing guarded owner invalidation and masks private history/proof; a stale failure cannot report against a replacement. Hold the actual pending read through settlement; no Promise.race, polling or replacement page queue.
7. Paging itself never calls refresh(requestId), installRequest, preview, act, proof consumption/discard/renewal, marker write/remove, newRequestId or any POST. It must not change selectedId, acknowledgment, financial busy ownership, proofRevision, canonical newRequestAllowed or balance. Preserve existing quote-expiry timers and proof quarantine; page activity cannot renew their lifetime.
8. Expose discovered older IDs through an explicit existing-request consultation control. Its click remains a separate existing recovery workflow: first fresh uncursored canonical context and exact request GET, original immutable terms/BigInt floors checked, no new ID. Block a selection that would discard a conflicting retained/UNKNOWN correlation; keep the original request recoverable. Restore/check each observed request's floor instead of treating a different selection as a new blank floor. Existing deliberate proof quarantine belongs to this explicit selection, never to the page callback; a proof for another selected request is never reused. No financial action is dispatched by discovery/selection; confirm/recover/seal remain their separately authorized existing explicit actions.

A page, local marker absence, null nextCursor or successful lookup never creates permission for another withdrawal, implies zero balance or declares a retry safe. Known terminal states remain terminal for their original identity; UNKNOWN remains uncertain until an exact newer authoritative observation resolves it. Legitimate later NEW lifecycles still require the existing independent canonical/current-session/storage/proof gates, never a page-local heuristic.

### Cross-owner prerequisites and limits

No A route or C1 change is needed for the proposed fail-closed consumer under the currently verified reader. Central source arbitration must explicitly authorize only these four B paths and the private paging-versus-authority boundary before implementation. A should confirm that its current operator isolation, whole-scope aggregation and lexical keyset ordering remain the intended read contract; C1 validates shapes/correlation but does not itself guarantee sort order or a signed snapshot cursor.

If product requirements demand an atomic owner/revision-bound history snapshot, or acceptance of changed eligibility/terms at the same scopeRevision, the current cursor/comparator contract cannot supply that. Such work requires an explicit A/C1 revision/continuation contract decision, not a B comparator/helper relaxation. Same-revision canonical payload changes continue to fail closed. Cursor reads and confirmations do not prove live provider availability or database correctness.

The existing explicit refresh(requestId) discards transient proof and can retire a different positively resolved marker; it must not be used as the page-loader shortcut. The proposed paged selection admission must preserve original retained/UNKNOWN correlation and per-ID floors before handing off to that existing explicit workflow. This coupling is covered within WithdrawalSetup; no reauthentication/storage helper edit is requested.
