### Smallest display and deliberate read interaction

Add one private “Histórico de compras” section to FinanceSection using the existing visual language. Its first bounded page lists original purchase date, gift title, Pix/card, quoted nominal/commission/processing/guest total and truthful knowledge status. A deliberate “Ver histórico da compra” opens the original purchase's payment/receipt facts; “Carregar mais” reads one issued page. There is no search/export/refund/support/transfer action in this first slice, no new public page and no guest identity display.

Recommend two finite read projections so an order with several payments/effects cannot create an unbounded nested response: purchase index at most 25 rows; selected purchase detail at most 50 total factual rows per page. These limits and a 128 KiB strict JSON response cap are proposed A contract bounds, not current endpoints. Payment and effect rows retain distinct IDs and original purchase references; more than one payment is visible, never collapsed into one presumed success. An empty page ends only its cursor chain. No page establishes absence of old payments, obligations or reversals, and the client never sums pages into a balance.

Initial mount may read only the first index after verified owner authorization. All further pages, detail opening and retries are deliberate. A detail read verifies the original purchase header and returns its bounded facts; additional detail pages are appended separately from that canonical header. A changed scope/revision/header during confirmation rejects the batch and asks for an explicit refresh. No automatic traversal, polling, reconciliation or provider fetch is part of these readers.

### Proposed minimum C1 fields, provenance and nullability

These are design field requests for A, not exported DTOs or permission to edit C1. All objects must be strict, site/mode-correlated and bounded; no browser financial authority is accepted.

| Proposed field group | Producer and meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Page header: siteId, mode, scopeRevision, observedAt, nextCursor | Site and sandbox/live mode come from authenticated server scope. observedAt is the stored-read observation time, not a receipt occurrence time. scopeRevision must have a real persistence/consistency contract described below. nextCursor is opaque/null for navigation only. No request-selected operator/account/mode or credential field. |
| Immutable purchase: orderId, quoteId, giftId, titleSnapshot, method, currency=BRL, purchasedAt | Original giftOrder and validated quote.snapshot; purchasedAt is order.createdAt, not quote creation, provider payment date or bank date. Use snapshot title after gift edits/deactivation. IDs and original values cannot mutate under the same identity. Guest name/email/CPF/message and hostedUrl/recoveryToken/requestHash/tokenHash are unnecessary and excluded. |
| Original binding metadata: originalAccountId, originalOperatorUserId, destinationVersion | Only local scoped account/operator identifiers and original quote destination version, used for correlation rather than displayed bank details. Original receivingAccount row plus immutable snapshot must agree; missing legacy operator/binding yields UNBOUND/financial detail unavailable, never substitution with the viewer/current recipient. Provider account/operator/recipient/bank references remain server-only. A must confirm durable original operator binding; the quote does not contain an immutable operatorUserId field. |
| Quoted allocation: nominalCents, commissionCents, processingCents, totalCents, policyVersion | Exactly persisted original quote. Current commission policy, repricing, rounding or provider charges never replace them. Each integer stays within the existing cents domain; total equals the original nominal+commission+processing. This allocation is what was quoted, not the receipt or refundable balance. |
| Payment observation fact: local paymentId, attemptId, orderId, safe classification/reason, factObservedAt, grossCents/processingCents/commissionCents/recipientNetCents | Validated giftPaymentRecord bound to the original order/attempt/account/environment and trusted observation. All actual monetary fields and observation time retain genuine nullability; missing net/split remains unknown. CONFIRMED or AWAITING_PAYMENT does not become received. Normalize unknown provider states to uncertainty; do not expose raw provider payload/reference. The retained quoted allocation remains separate even when actual amounts diverge. |
| Historical receipt fact: local effectId, paymentId, orderId, amountCents, postedAt, sourceObservedAt, validated source classification | A's correlated GIFT_RECEIVED CREDIT/proof chain. amountCents is the recorded recipient credit; postedAt is local ledger posting time and sourceObservedAt the trusted payment observation. Neither is invented provider occurredAt or bank credit. The server validator, not a string provenance label, establishes receipt. Keep this immutable fact visible as historical when a newer unresolved event makes current knowledge UNKNOWN. |
| Knowledge/coverage: current knowledge, safe reason, payment/receipt/reversal/settlement availability | Explicitly separate current certainty from retained facts. A processed receipt can coexist with later unresolved payment/refund evidence. Missing producer/incomplete correlation is UNAVAILABLE or UNKNOWN; null facts/amounts do not mean zero, CLOSED_UNPAID, no obligation or safe retry. Empty arrays require AVAILABLE coverage and still describe only the queried records. |
| Refund/reversal facts, initially unavailable | A future accepted producer must supply stable fact ID, original payment/receipt, compensatesReceiptId where applicable, actual guest refund gross and actual recipient debit as distinct nullable verified amounts, posted/source times and proof-aware outcome. Requested/accepted/SUCCEEDED operation or REFUNDED provider enum is not fulfillment evidence. Never infer full refund, refunded commission/processing or recipient debit from quoted amounts or from a ledger link alone. No such positive fact is supplied until A's producer/projection contract closes. |
| Dispute and settlement, initially unavailable where unsupported | A trustworthy restrictive observation may show “em verificação/restrição”, but does not prove refunded/debited money or dispute resolution. No existing per-purchase allocator binds a withdrawal/bank receipt to this purchase; keep settlement/bank credit unavailable. Existing WithdrawalHistoryView remains separate. Do not allocate aggregate withdrawals or available balance across purchases locally. |

Suggested wording distinguishes “Valores da cotação”, “Valores observados no pagamento” and “Recebimento registrado no razão”. Unresolved current knowledge says “Há registro anterior; a situação atual está em verificação”. Until a reversal producer exists, show “Histórico de estornos indisponível”, never “R$0 estornado” or “Nenhum estorno”. A local/sandbox fixture remains labeled test data and cannot establish native provider receipt.

### Exact authority, historical scope and privacy boundary

Viewer authority is the actual immutable panelActorOwner object and verified snapshot, user/session/sessionEpoch, unexpired nonregressive clock, selected same-site active SITE_ADMIN membership with readFinance. The backend freshly resolves that membership, denies query/body-selected authority and checks it again at stored-read settlement. Receiving READY, a designated-recipient match and action reauthentication are not substitutes for this read policy. OWNER/assessor/support access and all refund mutations remain separate denied domains.

Existing readFinance is site-level; it does not yet specify the new multi-account/history dataset. **Requested A/root policy confirmation:** preserve that selected-site read policy and read the site's historical original account/operator tuples in the server-selected environment, including inactive/deactivated originals, without filtering to the current receiving account. Validate every original operator/account/destination binding independently; the viewer is not silently rewritten as the original recipient. This proposal does not grant another operator's data: A/root must explicitly confirm this historical visibility policy before C1/B implementation. If rejected, A must return the exact approved operator subset with a scope identifier, not let B invent a filter or infer global completeness from an empty subset.

All cursors/pages/details bind to the current viewer owner/snapshot/epoch, site, server-selected mode, approved historical dataset scope and canonical generation. Different original operators stay distinct row bindings; no cross-site/environment join or current-account replacement. No namespace, wallet/bank fields, credential data, webhook authentication material, native/raw proofs, provider references, signed URLs or unneeded guest PII enter C1, DOM, persistence, telemetry, clipboard or error text. History caches are ephemeral; no localStorage/sessionStorage write is needed.

### Canonical read, revision and observation-floor contract requested from A

1. Supply strict private purchase-index and detail-page schemas/response envelopes under A ownership, including the fields/nullable coverage above, schema bounds and a documented immutable-versus-mutable field partition. Candidate GET routes are `/v1/sites/:siteId/finance/purchases` and `/v1/sites/:siteId/finance/purchases/:orderId/history`; route names are requests, not installed exports. Optional ports under finance should receive trusted actor/access, approved site/mode/history scope, exact original orderId for detail and validated opaque cursor. They must not be siteId-only callbacks that lose original scope. Missing port/mode/policy stays unavailable.
2. Publish the backend non-mutating administrative read authorization and protected no-store HTTP contract before B mounting. Only cursor is a query input; reject duplicate/invalid cursor and operator/account/environment/authority selectors. Validate all reply rows/header against the trusted scope. Reading history must not request/consume a financial proof, touch operations/inbox processing/ledger, rotate a session/cookie or call a provider.
3. Use bounded stored read-only transactions/batched joins over original order, quote, attempt, payment, correlated inbox and ledger/compensation links. Validate complete correlation and current uncertainty for each selected purchase before slicing its detail facts; no N+1 provider calls or missing-inner-join receipt loss. A page can be bounded without hiding unsupported/unresolved facts. Broken/multiple/foreign associations, contradictory allocations or unsupported compensations remain explicit unknown/unavailable, not a partial success or invented total. Preserve historical reads after gift/site/account/destination deactivation while current viewer membership still authorizes them.
4. Provide a **genuine revision/order persistence contract**. Current giftOrder/quote/payment/ledger/inbox fields do not export a canonical history-scope revision or immutable sequence for all updates. Payment observations can update in place; inbox uncertainty can change independently; equal displayed millisecond times cannot order contradictory payloads. B must not invent a count, poll number, maximum timestamp, lexical ID or Date.now revision. Recommend a durable server-owned scope/purchase/record version advanced by relevant fact changes, or an equally precise approved consistency token plus native/per-record order contract. If this needs persistence columns/CAS/append-only observation history, A must name the exact separate schema/writer/migration grant. No new persistence assumption or migration follows from this proposal.
5. Define stable keyset order explicitly: recommended purchase ordering uses original persisted creation microseconds descending plus orderId as a deterministic tie-breaker; detail facts need an equally stable persisted ordering identity. Expose exact microseconds/revisions as canonical decimal strings, not truncated JavaScript Date or floating-point counters. Lexical tie-breaking is pagination order, never financial event causality. Cursors carry navigation position/current scope generation opaquely; authorization is independent of cursor text.
6. Give per-record current-knowledge and strongest historical floors. Immutable quote/binding/receipt/reversal IDs and payloads cannot be rewritten or resurrected. A strictly newer authoritative correlated observation can resolve UNKNOWN; a fresh response clock, missing rows or a changed unrelated order cannot. Same revision/order with contradictory payload is incomparable. Server confirmation and client commit compare exact detail facts with the newly confirmed canonical purchase floor as well as prior cache, preserving the accepted paging lesson. Separate GET confirmations are not an atomic remote snapshot.
7. Confirm existing receipt production/provenance integration separately from future reversal/settlement production. Preserve prior GIFT_RECEIVED credits, and keep newer authenticated non-PROCESSED events/UNKNOWN operations in current uncertainty. A future refund/dispute writer must retain provider/native fulfillment identity, original scope, exact actual monetary allocations and idempotent compensations; principal cannot be double-compensated, and additional verified fees/debts cannot be silently zeroed. Guest gross refund, commission/processing recovery and recipient ledger debit are separate facts. Current REFUND enums/links/fixtures do not close that prerequisite. First history source may proceed with reversal/settlement coverage explicitly UNAVAILABLE once index/receipt contracts close; it need not wait for native refund enablement, and must not fabricate positive/empty outcomes.

These requests belong to A's future C1/service/repository/HTTP/persistence boundary under root arbitration. Root queues them around A's current productive verification4 work; B sends no direct integration/status follow-up and starts no A work.

### Actual transport and native authorization

1. Build one private fixture-local app with explicit fictional URLs, a fixed synthetic clock, fake secret/proof key, standard native auth, the frozen composition and real business factories. Pass the composition's captured db/auth/now references into createApp unchanged; its reference/configuration checks must remain active. The distinct read-only auth instance is never mounted as an auth handler. The standard instance retains normal /v1/me and POST behavior.
2. Use the real apiRequest third-fetcher argument. The injected fetcher accepts only the expected /api/v1 path, removes the /api prefix once, constructs a Request at the fixed fictional API origin, preserves method/body/query/encoded path and returns the actual app.request response. apiRequest parses JSON and raises its actual ApiError. Attach only the fixture's private cookie jar and configured Admin Origin; negative cases omit or replace them deliberately. No listener or global network fetch is used. This bypasses the Admin BFF and browser cookie machinery and must be labeled accordingly.
3. Create the mounted owner with an identity callback that calls actual /v1/me through this bridge. Load it normally, derive selected access/site/sessionEpoch from its validated actual response, then mount the real ReceivingSetup or WithdrawalSetup with that owner and transport. Do not resolve the owner from a preconstructed actor or return a fake Me/context envelope.
4. Follow the installed native-auth fixture pattern: obtain the real native cookie name from auth.$context and HMAC-sign a fictional stored session token with the fake secret. The SDK's getSignedCookie and auth.api.getSession remain real. Only internalAdapter.findSession storage is synthetic and must look up the requested token, returning null for absent tokens; a correctly signed unknown token is not an actor. The composition's private read instance may be captured with the existing call-through factory spy pattern, which still invokes the real builder. Never stub native getSession, cookie verification, authHttp or resolveSiteAccess.
5. Real stored-current-session/user and membership queries read the driver state independently of the SDK adapter. Missing/expired/disabled/foreign fresh rows must deny even when the raw synthetic SDK record exists. Wrong signature, missing cookie, wrong Origin and correct-but-unknown token exercise actual HTTP rejection. An actual runtime crypto implementation may be supplied if the jsdom global lacks it, and restored afterward; no signature-verification substitute is acceptable.
6. Keep real password and proof behavior. Generate only a fictional password hash through the installed native context.password.hash, seed the credential row, and use createFinancialPasswordVerifier plus the real rate limiter and FinancialReauthentication factory. Mounted password submission obtains the actual HTTP-issued fr1 proof. Wrong password, expired/tampered/cross-purpose/site/actor/session proofs must fail the real verifier/readProof path. Fake passwords, keys, cookies and proof tokens remain transient and private to the fixture.

### Synthetic storage and financial composition

Use a bounded local stateful NodePg-compatible driver behind real Drizzle, adapting the existing repository/native fixture patterns rather than their canned outcomes. It must support actual projected rows/rowMode, parameters, current-session reads/activity writes, membership filtering, credential and verification-counter storage, transaction drafts/commit/rollback and the selected nomination/withdrawal tables. Reject unknown SQL and mismatched scope/parameters. Model a lost acknowledgment by persisting a synthetic COMMIT and then failing its response, preserving the actual stored original identities. This verifies service/repository composition over synthetic stored effects; it cannot prove PostgreSQL isolation, locking, constraints or crash durability.

Nomination uses createReceivingNominationRepository through createReceivingNominationComposition. Do not stub readNominationScope/reserveNomination. Assert original receiving-account, ACCOUNT_CREATE operation and audit identities/counts after the actual POST and actual context GET. No account-creation provider is installed or called. A pending reservation is not a created/READY receiving account. Unchanged-policy direct HTTP replay can exercise backend idempotency; the mounted pending form must not manufacture another POST. ### Accepted contract and three distinct displays

Use exactly the published giftPurchaseHistoryScope/Purchase/Payment/Receipt/Page/DetailPage/PageResponse/DetailResponse schemas and their eight corresponding GiftPurchaseHistory types. Strict response envelopes are `{history: GiftPurchaseHistoryPage}` and `{history: GiftPurchaseHistoryDetailPage}`. Every page carries strict scope `{siteId, mode, policy: SITE_HISTORY}`, consistency PER_READ, readObservedAtMicros, currentKnowledge UNKNOWN|UNAVAILABLE, sourceOrdering UNAVAILABLE and opaque nextCursor|null. No local generation, financial revision, KNOWN, balance/refundable amount or completeness field is added. Native sessionEpoch and an internal active-attempt token fence lifecycle only; neither orders financial facts.

The index shows original purchase/title/method/date and original quoted nominal, commission, processing and total cents. Detail keeps three panels separate: **Valores da cotação**, **Observação retida nesta consulta**, and **Recebimento histórico registrado no razão**. Quote values never become actual payment money; nullable actual fields show unavailable/not recorded, while genuine zero remains zero. A retained recordedState RECEIVED is an observation classification, not a posted receipt or bank credit. The current line remains **Situação financeira atual não confirmada** even beside a previously validated receipt. Reversals and bankSettlement are always UNAVAILABLE; no refund amount, zero-refund assertion, settlement outcome or summed balance is inferred.

Coverage AVAILABLE means that producer supplied that category for this read, not complete history or current certainty. Latest-read coverage/availability is displayed separately from prior retained facts. A receipt-only valid page is supported; it must not be discarded because the matching payment is absent on that page. UNBOUND detail follows the actual C1 rule: UNKNOWN cannot be manufactured for it; payments/receipts are unavailable and empty, with currentKnowledge UNAVAILABLE. The source string CORRELATED_STORED_POSTING checks structure only: A's accepted producer must establish its complete authenticated stored chain before B calls a receipt validated.

### Future signatures, no implementation or new wire

```text
HistoryViewerScope = {siteId, access: SiteAccess|null, sessionEpoch: number|null}
HistoryPorts = {owner?: existing panel actor owner,
  request?: (path, {signal?}?) => Promise<unknown>, now?: () => number}
createGiftPaymentHistoryController(viewerScope, onState, ports) => {
  loadFirstIndex(), loadMoreIndex(issuedCursor), openPurchase(orderId),
  loadMoreDetail(orderId, issuedCursor), retryFailedRead(),
  restartIndex(), restartDetail(orderId), dispose(), whenSettled()
}
All read actions return Promise<boolean>; dispose masks/aborts immediately;
whenSettled returns the owned client fetch/body-read/cancellation/local-parsing
settlement Promise; it cannot report producer or database drainage.
```

These are bounded prospective B signatures inside giftPaymentHistory.ts, not exported code or an API grant. Requests parse the exact C1 response schemas. Opening a purchase comes from its previously admitted original index header; the server independently authorizes the exact orderId. Client input never chooses operator/account/environment/history policy. Default future transport must link its actual child signal and enforce streamed response bytes before JSON parsing, including error bodies, through the frozen apiRequest third-fetcher seam within this client path. No apiClient.ts or other helper change is needed. Injected parsed transports remain declared test seams, not proof of physical cancellation or wire-size enforcement.

### Native owner, scope and private cache lifetime

Capture the actual verified owner object and immutable getSnapshot result, user/session identity, native sessionEpoch, selected site and matching active SITE_ADMIN membership/readFinance. Access props must agree with the captured native membership; a SITE_HISTORY label cannot authorize them. OWNER, assistant/support, revoked/foreign membership and expired/unverified owners are denied. Read authority does not require recipient designation, receiving READY or financial proof. Original operators/accounts/destinations remain server-private and may be inactive; no current-operator filter or private identity field is reconstructed by B.

First admitted server scope fixes mode/policy for this private owner scope. Different site/mode/policy responses cannot be appended. Authorization-scope replacement masks/discards old data and cursors and requires a fresh authorized first read; it is not navigation restart. Actual owner/snapshot/session/access replacement immediately masks/discards all previous purchases, receipt union, samples, conflicts and read metadata before effects; the new baseline is UNKNOWN with no reconstructed financial success. Denied/absent producer has unavailable presentation.

Use one ephemeral owner-scope holder in the composed history surface, separate from page arrays/detail selection. Its admitted facts and cumulative admission counter survive detail close/reopen, explicit navigation restart, same-owner transport-port replacement and StrictMode child/controller replacement while that authorized holder remains alive. Replacement controllers reuse this holder; their cleanup cannot reset its counter or erase its receipt union. Full holder disposal or authorization-scope loss clears private cache/counter/subscriptions; no claim survives full application/process reload. The client completion slot lives outside this replaceable private holder and remains occupied until actual client settlement, even after private data is discarded. No module-wide financial cache, localStorage/sessionStorage, public recovery capability, proof/financial marker, clipboard/export or polling is added. A transport-port replacement masks the old runtime until the new current frame/scope is validated; obsolete promises/callbacks cannot mutate its cache.

### Exact immutable equality and incomparable observations

Validate the entire strict page and its bound before constructing candidate maps; commit all accepted changes atomically after the final current-owner fence. Compare parsed field values, not input object-key order, locale sorting, lossy dates or a digest presumed to be a version.

| Identity / equality domain | Exact rule |
| --- | --- |
| Purchase, keyed by owner scope/orderId | All public fields match: siteId, mode, orderId, quoteId, giftId, titleSnapshot, method, BRL, purchasedAtMicros, destinationVersion, policyVersion, binding and all four quoted integer cents. Also retain quoteId-to-orderId uniqueness across reads. A giftId may legitimately recur across different purchases. A changed header/binding under the same original identity is a conflict, not repricing or an implicit legacy upgrade. |
| Historical receipt, keyed by owner scope/effectId | Exact effectId/paymentId/orderId/BRL/recipientCreditCents/postedAtMicros/sourceObservedAtMicros/source equality, matching its unchanged purchase header. Keep paymentId-to-effectId and effectId-to-purchase association consistent across pages; a different positive effect for the same credited payment is a conflict, not another amount to sum. Exact duplicates are idempotent. |
| Retained payment identity | paymentId/attemptId/orderId/BRL/createdAtMicros bind the original record. An incompatible identity binding rejects/quarantines the page. |
| Retained payment sample | Exact recordedState/observedAtMicros/nullability and gross/processing/commission/recipientNet cents identify a sample after its immutable binding. Equal payloads deduplicate. Different samples for that payment remain incomparable, including equal or larger observed times, later responses or stronger-looking states. Retain the earlier sample as historical and show distinct retained samples/conflict without a latest/current/success winner. |

Same immutable identity with a contradictory payload quarantines the whole candidate batch, retains the prior immutable floor and reports that this read is unavailable/conflicting. A larger readObservedAtMicros, cursor or request count never selects a winner. Scope-loss disposal is the exception: privacy requires discarding that old scope rather than retaining its display.

Receipt union is keyed by the same private owner scope and original purchase. Missing receipts, empty arrays, nextCursor:null, unavailable coverage, ordinary failures, retry or explicit index/detail restart never delete it. New valid effects append after equality/correlation checks. The current per-read page and previously admitted union are different objects: **never merge them into a fictitious C1 detail page and revalidate that aggregate as one query**. C1 may reject a same-page inconsistent payment/receipt pair; across separate valid reads, a different retained payment sample can coexist with an earlier historical receipt without rewriting either or certifying current state. Prior receipt display is labeled as retained history; the new read's unavailable/conflicting category is still visible.

### Deliberate cursor interaction and bounds

Initial authorized mount reads at most one first index page. A deliberate detail open issues one first detail GET; index/detail load-more issues exactly the last privately admitted cursor for its captured scope and original orderId. Retry repeats only the failed still-owned GET. Explicit restart must reserve a read admission before resetting that stream's navigation/issued-cursor set and displayed page selection; it preserves same-owner immutable maps, receipt union, incomparable samples and cumulative admission count. A denied reservation changes none of that bookkeeping. Restart neither certifies a newer generation nor restores current certainty. No queued click, traversal, background refresh or automatic follow-up request is allowed.

Cursor tokens, including accepted producer gh1 tokens, are opaque, bounded C1 strings. Encode path segments and cursor exactly, including dot escaping required by the frozen API-path guard; never decode cursor contents or infer authority/order from their encoding. Reject same-chain token reuse/cycles and foreign original-purchase/scope continuations before any append. Repeated known immutable records must match exactly; an overlapping continuation is not another purchase/receipt. Index navigation follows descending purchasedAtMicros and descending lexical orderId; cross-page append must preserve that boundary. Root12:02 accepted A's detail comparator: microseconds ASC, kind PAYMENT0 before RECEIPT1, then lexical local ID ASC. Use public createdAtMicros/paymentId for PAYMENT and postedAtMicros/effectId for RECEIPT. This comparator governs navigation boundaries only; it does not order native financial observations or select a current payment sample.

An empty page preserves all prior facts and shows only that this query yielded no rows. C1 allows an empty page with a different valid continuation; if supplied, expose its next deliberate action without automatically skipping it or treating it as completeness. Null cursor ends that navigation chain only. PER_READ allows separately queried pages to differ; there is no head-confirmation/revision handshake or atomic all-pages snapshot. Every repeated original header and receipt still requires exact equality.

Honor giftPurchaseHistoryLimits: at most25 index rows, at most50 **combined** retained-payment plus historical-receipt facts per detail response, and131072 UTF-8 JSON bytes, including the strict `{history}` envelope. Enforce actual streamed body bytes/time and parsed C1 bounds; declared Content-Length or JavaScript string length alone is insufficient. Reject an over-bound response rather than truncate it. Those are per-response limits, not permission to truncate a cross-page receipt union to50 or silently evict known historical facts.

The minimum local design candidate is MAX_OWNER_READ_ADMISSIONS =64 in the persistent authorized owner-scope holder. Charge once before each dispatched read: first index/detail, either load-more, retry and restart, including empty, duplicate-only, failed or aborted reads. Never refund a dispatched read or reset its count during same-owner navigation, port replacement or StrictMode replay. The64th admission may dispatch; the65th may not. Exhaustion blocks new dispatch and displays local unavailability while preserving all admitted purchases, receipts, incomparable samples and cursor facts. No silent eviction, global configuration or completeness claim is introduced.

This bounds cumulative admitted index purchases to1600, detail facts/samples to3200, response payload to8MiB and retained read/cursor records to64; these are conservative logical payload/cardinality bounds, not a measured JavaScript heap bound. Keep at most one response staging buffer capped at131072 bytes and no unbounded failed-read log. Exact duplicates may reduce retained counts but never refund admission. The admission counter fences local work only; it does not order financial observations or create financial authority.

### Actual completion and composition

One history read slot outside the replaceable private holder owns actual client fetch, body-read/cancellation and local parsing settlement across keyed view replacement. A proposed45000ms local feedback deadline may report unavailability/request abort; abort/disposal/timeout masks callbacks but cannot free the slot before that client work actually settles. Enforce streamed bytes before JSON parsing for success and error responses. Browser abort and whenSettled cannot observe or certify producer/database completion: A's accepted producer/HTTP independently owns backend transaction/driver completion, including work continuing after client abort. No Promise.race establishes completion.

Recheck current owner/snapshot/access, captured request/clock ports, selected original purchase and issued cursor before dispatch, after awaits/external callbacks, before staging and before final map/state assignment. Controller cleanup aborts and detaches its subscriptions while preserving the same authorized holder's facts/counter; full holder disposal or authorization loss clears private scope immediately. whenSettled awaits only the owned client settlement. Current401/403 uses the existing captured-owner failure policy; obsolete failures cannot invalidate a replacement owner.

FinanceSection composes only this read surface. History reads/errors/restarts create no marker/operation ID, consume no proof, alter no original proof deadline/financial busy state and dispatch no nomination/withdrawal/preview/confirmation/recovery/seal/refund/transfer/provider action. A genuine current native authorization loss can invalidate the common owner as usual; ordinary availability or immutable conflicts do not reauthenticate or quarantine an unrelated financial form. Stable existing finance children/keys and withdrawal ownership remain preserved.

### Exact history-only path policy

First classify/reserve the history resource FAMILY before method and site/order/suffix/query/framing validity; do not require a valid tuple to recognize that family. History-family requests with unsupported methods terminate locally405, with no body read or upstream fetch. Malformed site/original/suffix/query/body/framing or encoding terminates locally with a fixed sanitized denial, without falling through to ordinary request.clone().arrayBuffer(), JSON text() or unowned response forwarding. The encoded-original exception is then limited to GET/HEAD of /api/v1/sites/<canonical-site>/finance/purchases or /api/v1/sites/<canonical-site>/finance/purchases/<original-order>/history. Fixed literals, segment count and trailing suffix are exact; the site remains the unchanged siteIdSchema grammar with no encoded aliases. For the new encoded-order lane, decode ONLY that segment once, validate with the accepted giftPurchaseHistoryPurchaseSchema orderId grammar, then require exact canonical re-encoding. Select encodeURIComponent of the original ID with every dot escaped as uppercase %2E; colon becomes uppercase %3A. This represents an original such as order:a..b without any literal double-dot in the frozen apiRequest path. Reject malformed escapes, lowercase/noncanonical encoded aliases, unnecessary encoded alphanumerics, decoded percent/double encoding, slash/backslash/separators, standalone dot traversal and encoded site/static prefixes. An embedded double-dot in a valid alphanumeric-leading original ID is data inside that one canonical encoded segment, not a navigation segment.

Retain its encoding for Hono's independent parameter hop; do not concatenate a decoded original into a URL or recursively decode. ### Bounded history success/error and actual ownership

Select a dedicated history branch after trusted resolution; ordinary non-history JSON forwarding, native-auth secret stripping/cookies, reports and mediaUpstream remain untouched. The history branch must never use upstream.text()/arrayBuffer() or pass an unowned upstream body through. Each job captures actual fetch ownership, a child AbortController, request abort linkage, response reader and a single observed cancellation promise. ### Actual ownership and caller lifecycle boundary

The private async request can hold one captured fetch promise, a per-job AbortController, response reader, pending read and one observed cancellation promise. The minimal API pair cannot observe those guards during transport because no public method accepts a current/signal token and the API has no dispose/whenSettled interface. ### Server search, coherent count and capacity

Run the literal contiguous predicate on the SERVER before count/order/offset/limit: match in title OR description independently, never across a concatenated boundary. Preserve accents, canonical NFC and Portuguese lowercase semantics from A; %, underscore, quote and backslash remain literal parameter values, with no wildcard/regex/relevance/token/accent removal. Prefer bound strpos/POSITION predicates over wildcard ILIKE. Do not fetch the full catalog into the API/client and filter/page it afterward.

A conditional normalize(...,NFC)/lower(... COLLATE a verified Portuguese collation) SQL predicate is feasible only after that profile is established. Its presence/locale behavior was NOT probed here. Do not quietly substitute default database lower, JS-only page filtering or a persisted normalization column. If unavailable, root must narrow the shared search semantics or grant one proven collation/search-representation migration; no such migration is established now.

Use one parameterized SQL statement/snapshot for private capacity count, matching PUBLISHED count and selected ordered rows. A count CTE plus a LEFT JOIN/LATERAL limited page supplies an empty-page sentinel and truthful counts even when offset has no rows; COUNT OVER alone cannot supply metadata for an empty page. Scope every count/predicate to the captured site, with PUBLISHED on matching/page data. Order sort_order ASC then ASCII id ASC under binary C ordering. Offset20*(page-1); validate numeric/count/order/site/card cardinality against C1. Hydrate only selected page media, never every matched item. A selected invalid media/card makes the entire page unavailable, without skipping/shrinking. No draft/archive occupancy or private media/provider/proof is exposed.

Under A's alternative put capacity<=200 admission in that same snapshot and return generic503 before any public page; do not return filtered-count success from an over-capacity source. Under B's alternative retain readable legacy pages and report actual matching count, while growth admission uses active occupancy. Counts/order are coherent WITHIN a read, not an immutable snapshot across separate page requests; no financial/source revision is fabricated.

Creation guard counts after acquiring the existing site row lock, inside the same READ COMMITTED transaction before insert; a waiting creator's new statement sees the preceding committed admission. With B's policy, restoration checks the current archived state and active count under that same lock before publication. Maintain existing pre/post-lock current manageCatalog, receiving/site/demo/term/media gates. Preserve original gift IDs/prices, quote snapshots, receiving authority and all UNKNOWN/terminal/action locks. ### Reproducible frozen verification for the READ_ONLY reviewer

Run this installed TypeScript AST/hash/whitespace checker in the existing workspace; it reads source as text, including the DB file, and does NOT import or execute a DB suite:
```sh
bun - <<'JS'

import ts from "typescript";
import {createHash} from "node:crypto";
import {readFileSync} from "node:fs";
const digest=value=>createHash("sha256").update(value).digest("hex");
const printer=ts.createPrinter({newLine:ts.NewLineKind.LineFeed});
const inspect=text=>{
 const file=ts.createSourceFile("source.tsx",text,ts.ScriptTarget.Latest,true,ts.ScriptKind.TSX);
 const functions={}, cases={};
 function visit(node){
  let name;
  if(ts.isFunctionDeclaration(node)||ts.isMethodDeclaration(node)||ts.isTypeAliasDeclaration(node))name=node.name?.getText(file);
  if(ts.isConstructorDeclaration(node))name="constructor";
  if(ts.isJsxElement(node)&&node.openingElement.tagName.getText(file)==="SiteDialog")name="SiteDialog";
  if(ts.isIfStatement(node)&&/resource === "gift-(quotes|checkouts)/.test(node.expression.getText(file)))name=node.expression.getText(file);
  if(name)functions[name]=digest(printer.printNode(ts.EmitHint.Unspecified,node,file));
  if(ts.isCallExpression(node)){
    const callee=node.expression.getText(file).replace(/\s+/g,"");
    if((callee==="it"||callee.startsWith("it.each("))&&node.arguments[0]&&(ts.isStringLiteral(node.arguments[0])||ts.isNoSubstitutionTemplateLiteral(node.arguments[0])))
      cases[node.arguments[0].text]=digest(printer.printNode(ts.EmitHint.Unspecified,node,file));
  }
  ts.forEachChild(node,visit);
 }
 visit(file);return{functions,cases};
};
```
